Sequence\n        values (``requires``, ``inputs``, ``expects``, ``parameterize``) are\n        normalised to tuples; nested mappings inside ``inputs`` / ``expects``\n        / ``parameterize`` rows are passed through as-is (the schema already\n        constrained their shape).\n\n        Missing optional keys fall back to the dataclass defaults; ``id`` and\n        ``title`` are required by the schema upstream so absence here is a\n        contract bug rather than user error.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if \"id\" not in data or \"title\" not in data:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"EvalSpec.from_dict requires schema-validated input with 'id' and 'title'\"\n            )\n\n        requires_raw = data.get(\"requires\", ())\n        inputs_raw = data.get(\"inputs\", ())\n        expects_raw = data.get(\"expects\", ())\n        parameterize_raw = data.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n\n        return cls(\n            id=data[\"id\"],\n            title=data[\"title\"],\n            category=data.get(\"category\", \"\"),\n            requires=tuple(requires_raw),\n            timeout_sec=data.get(\"timeout_sec\"),\n            isolation=data.get(\"isolation\"),\n            inputs=tuple(inputs_raw),\n            expects=tuple(expects_raw),\n            parameterize=tuple(parameterize_raw),\n        )\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ExpectResult:\n    \"\"\"Assertion outcome returned by every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-009).\n\n    Six-field contract:\n\n    * ``name``         — assertion name, matching the DSL method that emitted\n      the result (e.g. ``\"exit_code\"``, ``\"file.contains_event\"``). Used by\n      the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) as a stable per-Expect key.\n    * ``passed``       — ``True`` when the assertion was satisfied. The\n      reporter rolls these up into the per-eval pass/fail bit.\n    * ``message``      — human-readable one-liner suitable for the terminal\n      summary; empty string when no message is required.\n    * ``details``      — additional structured information (typically the\n      diff fragments or matched event payload) carried through to the JSON\n      report. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass\n      keeps its equality / hashability contract; an empty mapping is the\n      default for passing results.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds the assertion took to evaluate.\n      Stored as ``float`` to capture sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``failure``      — optional ``ExpectFailure`` (DM-005 / T01.16) carrying\n      the diff-level detail for a failing assertion. ``None`` for passing\n      results; DM-009 does not couple the failure presence to ``passed``, so\n      callers must populate ``failure`` explicitly when they want the\n      reporter to render a rich per-Expect failure block.\n\n    Equality is structural across all six fields (``@dataclass``-generated\n    ``__eq__``). The dataclass is frozen so once an ``ExpectCallable``\n    returns a result it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    passed: bool\n    message: str = \"\"\n    details: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n    failure: Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"] = None\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-009.\n\n        Uses ``dataclasses.asdict`` so nested dataclasses (notably\n        ``ExpectFailure`` once T01.16 lands) are recursively unwrapped into\n        plain dicts. ``details`` is shallow-copied to break the shared\n        reference with the dataclass instance — callers that mutate the\n        returned mapping must not affect the immutable source.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload = dataclasses.asdict(self)\n        # ``asdict`` already returns a fresh mapping, but ``details`` may\n        # contain nested mutable containers (lists of event payloads, etc.).\n        # We rely on ``asdict``'s recursive copy for that — no extra work\n        # needed here. Keep this method as the one stable serialisation\n        # surface so the Reporter can call it without touching internals.\n        return payload\n\n\n# Field order below mirrors DM-005 so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n# Reporter snapshots and review diffs.\n_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"expect_id\",\n    \"expect_name\",\n    \"expected\",\n    \"actual\",\n    \"message\",\n    \"artifact_ref\",\n    \"traceback\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ExpectFailure:\n    \"\"\"Per-Expect failure detail record (DM-005).\n\n    The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) attaches exactly one ``ExpectFailure``\n    to each failing assertion inside ``EvalOutcome`` (T03.01). The 8-field\n    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n\n    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. Same\n      typing rationale as ``expected``.\n    * ``message``     — human-readable one-liner summarising the diff;\n      defaults to ``\"\"`` so a caller can omit it when the diff between\n      ``expected`` / ``actual`` is self-explanatory.\n    * ``artifact_ref`` — optional path-or-URI to a side-car artifact\n      (rendered diff file, dumped JSONL, captured stderr). ``None`` when no\n      artifact was written. The Reporter resolves the ref against the run's\n      scratch root before emitting the JSON report.\n    * ``traceback``   — optional Python traceback string captured when the\n      Expect itself raised. ``None`` for assertion failures that produced a\n      clean diff rather than an exception.\n\n    The dataclass is frozen so a Reporter consumer cannot mutate failure\n    records mid-render. Equality is structural across all 8 fields, which\n    keeps deduplication straightforward.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    expect_id: str\n    expect_name: str\n    expected: Any\n    actual: Any\n    message: str = \"\"\n    artifact_ref: Optional[str] = None\n    traceback: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-005.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`` so\n        the output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or\n        dataclass internals. ``expected`` / ``actual`` are passed through\n        as-is — callers populate them with JSON-shaped payloads, so the\n        reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result directly.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS}\n","numLines":233,"startLine":1,"totalLines":233}}}

Default empty when the `expected`/`actual` diff is self-explanatory. |\n| 7 | `artifact_ref` | `Optional[str]`  | `None`  | Path-or-URI to a side-car artifact (rendered diff, dumped JSONL, stderr). `None` when no artifact was written. |\n| 8 | `traceback`    | `Optional[str]`  | `None`  | Captured Python traceback string when the Expect itself raised. `None` for clean-diff assertion failures. |\n\n## Invariants\n\n- `@dataclass(frozen=True)` — mutation raises `dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError` (`test_expect_failure_is_frozen`).\n- Field declaration order matches DM-005 verbatim; `to_dict()` enforces the same ordering through an explicit field tuple so the output is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass internals (`test_expect_failure_to_dict_field_order_matches_dm005`).\n- Two instances built from identical arguments compare equal via the `@dataclass`-generated `__eq__` (`test_expect_failure_deterministic_equality`).\n- One `ExpectFailure` entry per failing Expect: the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) constructs a fresh record per assertion. The phase-1 tasklist explicitly names this module as the destination (\"`src/superclaude/cli/eval/models.py` with the 8 fields from DM-005\"), and T01.15's `Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"]` forward annotation was authored on the assumption the concrete class would land in the same module. Co-locating the pair removes the forward reference at evaluation time without needing a `TYPE_CHECKING` dance.\n6\t\n7\t2. **`expected` / `actual` typed as `Any`.** DM-005 does not constrain payload shape, and primitive Expects (COMP-010.1–6, M4) will populate these with arbitrary JSON-shaped values (int exit codes, regex strings, dict event payloads). Narrower typing now would force a refactor when primitives land. `Any` matches the DM-005 spirit (\"assertion failure detail\") and keeps the contract permissive.\n8\t\n9\t3. **`message` defaults to `\"\"`.** Symmetric with `ExpectResult.message` from T01.15 — callers should be able to omit the message when the `expected`/`actual` diff is self-explanatory. The Reporter renders the empty string as \"no summary message\" rather than treating absence as an error.\n10\t\n11\t4. **`artifact_ref` and `traceback` typed as `Optional[str]` with default `None`.** DM-005 does not state these are required. The Reporter generates artifact refs as part of writing the JSON report (post-construction), so the dataclass must accept the missing case at construction time. Same reasoning for `traceback`: only the exception path captures it.\n12\t\n13\t5. **`to_dict()` builds the dict explicitly from `_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`.** Chose this over `dataclasses.asdict(self)` to guarantee ordering. `asdict` returns insertion order which currently matches field-declaration order, but the spec demands a stable contract for reporter snapshots, and an explicit field tuple makes the guarantee visible. The test `test_expect_failure_to_dict_field_order_matches_dm005` locks this. `test_expect_failure_asdict_matches_to_dict` documents that the two agree on values today (ordering is owned by `to_dict`).\n14\t\n15\t6. **No `__post_init__` validation.** DM-005 does not require value-level checks (unlike DM-007 `Capability.failure_mode` from T01.09 which enumerated literals). Adding validation would risk false negatives — the Reporter is the right layer to decide what \"looks empty\" means for an `expected` payload of, say, `None` or `0`.\n16\t\n17\t7. **Forward reference resolution for `ExpectResult.failure`.** T01.15 already accommodates an `Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"]` forward annotation via `from __future__ import annotations`. T01.16 lands the concrete class in the same module, so the annotation now resolves to a real type at runtime. No production-code change is needed in `ExpectResult`; the test stand-in from T01.15 is superseded by `test_expect_failure_round_trips_inside_expect_result` here, which exercises the genuine `ExpectFailure` inside an `ExpectResult`.\n18\t\n19\t8. **Package re-export.** Added `ExpectFailure` to `src/superclaude/cli/eval/__init__.py`'s `__all__` so callers can `from superclaude.cli.eval import ExpectFailure` — symmetric with `ExpectResult` and `EvalSpec`. Locked by `test_expect_failure_importable_from_package`.\n20\t\n21\t9. **Module docstring updated.** Listed DM-005 / D-0014 / T01.16 alongside DM-002 / DM-009 and described the 8-field ExpectFailure contract so the file's purpose statement stays accurate.\n22\t\n23\t## Things deliberately NOT in scope of T01.16\n24\t\n25\t- Real `ExpectCallable` primitives that emit populated `ExpectFailure`s — M4 (COMP-010.1–6).\n26\t- Reporter side-effects: artifact ref resolution against the run's scratch root, JSONL writes, terminal rendering — COMP-008 / T03.13.\n27\t- `EvalOutcome` aggregation of multiple `ExpectFailure`s per eval — T03.01.\n28\t- A \"two failing Expects in a single eval → two ExpectFailure entries\" *integration* test — that lives in the Reporter test set / T03.13. T01.16 covers the per-failure construction pattern (`test_expect_failure_two_per_eval_pattern`).\n29\t\n30\t## Risks observed during build\n31\t\n32\t- **`Any` typing on `expected` / `actual`.** Permissive by design, but a future strict-typing pass may want a generic narrowing once primitive payload shapes are known (M4). The dataclass is frozen, so any tightening will be a non-breaking annotation-only change.\n33\t- **`to_dict()` ordering vs. asdict().** Two serialisation paths exist (`to_dict()` and `dataclasses.asdict`). They agree on values today; only `to_dict()` guarantees ordering. The Reporter (T03.13) must call `to_dict()` — `asdict` is acceptable for ad-hoc tests but not for snapshot diffs. Narrower typing now would force a refactor when primitives land. `Any` matches the DM-005 spirit (\"assertion failure detail\") and keeps the contract permissive.\n\n3. **`message` defaults to `\"\"`.** Symmetric with `ExpectResult.message` from T01.15 — callers should be able to omit the message when the `expected`/`actual` diff is self-explanatory. The Reporter renders the empty string as \"no summary message\" rather than treating absence as an error.\n\n4. **`artifact_ref` and `traceback` typed as `Optional[str]` with default `None`.** DM-005 does not state these are required. The Reporter generates artifact refs as part of writing the JSON report (post-construction), so the dataclass must accept the missing case at construction time. Same reasoning for `traceback`: only the exception path captures it.\n\n5. **`to_dict()` builds the dict explicitly from `_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`.** Chose this over `dataclasses.asdict(self)` to guarantee ordering. `asdict` returns insertion order which currently matches field-declaration order, but the spec demands a stable contract for reporter snapshots, and an explicit field tuple makes the guarantee visible. The test `test_expect_failure_to_dict_field_order_matches_dm005` locks this. `test_expect_failure_asdict_matches_to_dict` documents that the two agree on values today (ordering is owned by `to_dict`).\n\n6. **No `__post_init__` validation.** DM-005 does not require value-level checks (unlike DM-007 `Capability.failure_mode` from T01.09 which enumerated literals). Adding validation would risk false negatives — the Reporter is the right layer to decide what \"looks empty\" means for an `expected` payload of, say, `None` or `0`.\n\n7. **Forward reference resolution for `ExpectResult.failure`.** T01.15 already accommodates an `Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"]` forward annotation via `from __future__ import annotations`. T01.16 lands the concrete class in the same module, so the annotation now resolves to a real type at runtime. No production-code change is needed in `ExpectResult`; the test stand-in from T01.15 is superseded by `test_expect_failure_round_trips_inside_expect_result` here, which exercises the genuine `ExpectFailure` inside an `ExpectResult`.\n\n8. **Package re-export.** Added `ExpectFailure` to `src/superclaude/cli/eval/__init__.py`'s `__all__` so callers can `from superclaude.cli.eval import ExpectFailure` — symmetric with `ExpectResult` and `EvalSpec`. Locked by `test_expect_failure_importable_from_package`.\n\n9. **Module docstring updated.** Listed DM-005 / D-0014 / T01.16 alongside DM-002 / DM-009 and described the 8-field ExpectFailure contract so the file's purpose statement stays accurate.\n\n## Things deliberately NOT in scope of T01.16\n\n- Real `ExpectCallable` primitives that emit populated `ExpectFailure`s — M4 (COMP-010.1–6).\n- Reporter side-effects: artifact ref resolution against the run's scratch root, JSONL writes, terminal rendering — COMP-008 / T03.13.\n- `EvalOutcome` aggregation of multiple `ExpectFailure`s per eval — T03.01.\n- A \"two failing Expects in a single eval → two ExpectFailure entries\" *integration* test — that lives in the Reporter test set / T03.13. T01.16 covers the per-failure construction pattern (`test_expect_failure_two_per_eval_pattern`).\n\n## Risks observed during build\n\n- **`Any` typing on `expected` / `actual`.** Permissive by design, but a future strict-typing pass may want a generic narrowing once primitive payload shapes are known (M4). The dataclass is frozen, so any tightening will be a non-breaking annotation-only change.\n- **`to_dict()` ordering vs. asdict().** Two serialisation paths exist (`to_dict()` and `dataclasses.asdict`). They agree on values today; only `to_dict()` guarantees ordering. The Reporter (T03.13) must call `to_dict()` — `asdict` is acceptable for ad-hoc tests but not for snapshot diffs. The spec.md *Serialisation* section documents this.\n","numLines":34,"startLine":1,"totalLines":34}}}

Co-\n   locating the dataclass with the methods that operate on it keeps the\n   import surface clean and avoids a circular import between `models` and\n   the future `containment_guard` (which would otherwise need to import\n   the record from `models`).\n\n2. **`__post_init__` calls `validate_eval_id`.** DM-006 does not\n   explicitly mandate validation in the dataclass, but the T02.04\n   acceptance criteria require \"Construction with an unsafe `eval_id`\n   raises `InvalidEvalId` (delegated to `validate_eval_id`)\". Delegating\n   to the existing FR-SCH2 guard (T01.05) rather than re-implementing\n   the regex avoids drift: there is one source of truth for the\n   acceptable-id surface, and any future tightening of the regex flows\n   through to this dataclass automatically.\n\n3. **`home_root` typed as `pathlib.Path`, stored verbatim.** DM-006\n   declares the field as `Path`. The dataclass does not call\n   `resolve()`, `expanduser()`, or any containment check at construction\n   time — that is FR-ISO2's job (T02.08) and adding it here would force\n   the unit tests to mkdtemp a real directory just to construct a record,\n   which slows the test suite and conflates the record contract with the\n   guard contract.\n\n4. **`time_offset_sec` kept regardless of OQ-8 state.** The DOC-OQ8\n   resolution (T06.03) decides whether `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET` is\n   actually plumbed through to the subprocess env. Either way the\n   record's contract is stable — the field stays at type `int`, default\n   `0`. If OQ-8 closes \"no\", downstream `HomeIsolation.env()` (T02.07)\n   simply omits the env var; the dataclass surface does not need to\n   change.\n\n5. **No `to_dict()` method.** Unlike `ExpectResult` / `ExpectFailure`,\n   `HomeIsolation` is not serialised into the JSON report. It is an\n   internal handle threaded through the orchestrator → runner pipeline.\n   Adding `to_dict()` now would be speculative; if a reporter ever needs\n   to render isolation state (e.g. for a \"what HOME ran this eval\" line)\n   the addition will be non-breaking (frozen-dataclass plus a new\n   method).\n\n6. **Re-validation cost is acceptable.** `validate_eval_id` does a\n   single compiled-regex `fullmatch` plus a type check.
